   Appearance

   From Wikipedia, the free encyclopedia

   "1,000", "Thousand", and "Chiliad" redirect here. For other uses, see
   [172]1000 (disambiguation) or, for the military unit, see
   [173]Chiliarchy.

   Natural number
   [174]← 999 1000 [175]1001 →
     * [176]List of numbers
     * [177]Integers

   [178]← [179]0 1k [180]2k [181]3k [182]4k [183]5k [184]6k [185]7k

   [190]Ordinal 1000th
   (one thousandth)
   [191]Factorization 2^3 × 5^3
   [192]Divisors 1, 2, 4, 5, 8, 10, 20, 25, 40, 50, 100, 125, 200, 250,
   500, 1000

   [203]Binary 1111101000[2]
   [204]Ternary 1101001[3]

   [209]Tamil ௲

   Look up [215]thousand or [216]1000 in Wiktionary, the free dictionary.

   1000 or one thousand is the [217]natural number following [218]999 and
   preceding [219]1001. In most [220]English-speaking countries, it can be
   written with or without a comma or sometimes a period [221]separating
   the thousands digit: 1,000.

   A group of one thousand things is sometimes known, from [222]Ancient
   Greek, as a chiliad.^[223][1] A period of one thousand years may be
   known as a chiliad or, more often from [224]Latin, as a
   [225]millennium. The number 1000 is also sometimes described as a short
   thousand in medieval contexts where it is necessary to distinguish the
   [226]Germanic concept of 1200 as a [227]long thousand. It is the first
   4-digit [228]integer.

Notation

   [[229]edit]

     * The decimal representation for one thousand is
          + 1000—a [230]one followed by three [231]zeros, in the general
            notation;
          + 1 × 10^3—in [232]engineering notation, which for this number
            coincides with:
          + 1 × 10^3 exactly—in [233]scientific normalized exponential
            notation;
          + 1 E+3 exactly—in [234]scientific E notation.
     * The [235]SI prefix for a thousand units is "[236]kilo-",
       abbreviated to "k"—for instance, a [237]kilogram or "kg" is a
       thousand [238]grams. This is sometimes extended to non-SI contexts,
       such as "ka" ([239]kiloannum) being used as a shorthand for periods
       of 1000 years. In [240]computer science, however, "kilo-" is used
       more loosely to mean 2 to the 10th power (1024).
     * In the [241]SI writing style, a [242]non-breaking space can be used
       as a [243]thousands separator, i.e., to separate the digits of a
       number at every power of 1000.
     * Multiples of thousands are occasionally represented by replacing
       their last three zeros with the letter "K" or "k": for instance,
       writing "$30k" for $30 000 or denoting the [244]Y2K computer bug of
       the year 2000.
     * A thousand units of [245]currency, especially [246]dollars or
       [247]pounds, are colloquially called a grand. In the United States,
       this is sometimes abbreviated with a "G" suffix.

In Mathematics

   [[248]edit]

   A [249]chiliagon is a 1000-sided [250]polygon.^[251][2]

Numbers in the range 1001–1999

   [[252]edit]

1001 to 1099

   [[253]edit]

     * [254]1001 = [255]sphenic number (7 × 11 × 13), [256]pentagonal
       number, [257]pentatope number, [258]palindromic number
     * 1002 = sphenic number, [259]Mertens function zero, [260]abundant
       number, number of [261]partitions of 22
     * 1003 = the product of some prime p and the p^th prime, namely p =
       17.
     * 1004 = [262]heptanacci number^[263][3]
     * 1005 = Mertens function zero, decagonal pyramidal number^[264][4]
     * 1006 = [265]semiprime, product of two distinct [266]isolated primes
       (2 and 503); [267]unusual number; [268]square-free number; number
       of [269]compositions (ordered [270]partitions) of 22 into squares;
       sum of two distinct [271]pentatope numbers (5 and 1001); number of
       undirected [272]Hamiltonian paths in 4 by 5 [273]square grid
       graph;^[274][5] record gap between [275]twin primes;^[276][6]
       number that is the sum of 7 positive 5th powers.^[277][7] In
       decimal: [278]equidigital number; when turned around, the number
       looks like a prime, 9001; its [279]cube can be [280]concatenated
       from other cubes, 1_0_1_8_1_0_8_216 ("_" indicates concatenation, 0
       = 0^3, 1 = 1^3, 8 = 2^3, 216 = 6^3)^[281][8]
     * 1007 = number that is the sum of 8 positive 5th powers^[282][9]
     * 1008 = divisible by the number of primes below it
     * 1009 = smallest four-digit [283]prime, [284]palindromic in bases
       11, 15, 19, 24 and 28: (838[11], 474[15], 2F2[19], 1I1[24],
       181[28]). It is also a [285]Lucky prime and [286]Chen prime.
     * 1010 = 10^3 + 10,^[287][10] Mertens function zero
     * 1011 = the largest n such that 2^n contains 101 and does not
       contain 11011, [288]Harshad number in bases 5, 10, 15, 20, 25, 30,
       35, 40, 45, 50, 55, 60, 65, 70, 75 (and 202 other bases), number of
       partitions of 1 into reciprocals of positive integers <= 16
       [289]Egyptian fraction^[290][11]
     * 1012 = [291]ternary number, (32[10]) quadruple [292]triangular
       number ([293]triangular number is [294]253),^[295][12] number of
       partitions of 1 into reciprocals of positive integers <= 17
       [296]Egyptian fraction^[297][11]
     * 1013 = [298]Sophie Germain prime,^[299][13] [300]centered square
       number,^[301][14] Mertens function zero
     * 1014 = 2^10-10,^[302][15] Mertens function zero, sum of the
       nontriangular numbers between successive triangular numbers 78 and
       91^[303][16]
     * 1015 = [304]square pyramidal number^[305][17]
     * 1016 = member of the [306]Mian–Chowla sequence,^[307][18]
       [308]stella octangula number, number of surface points on a cube
       with edge-length 14^[309][19]
     * 1017 = generalized triacontagonal number^[310][20]
     * 1018 = Mertens function zero, 1018^16 + 1 is prime^[311][21]
     * 1019 = [312]Sophie Germain prime,^[313][13] [314]safe
       prime,^[315][22] [316]Chen prime
     * 1020 = [317]polydivisible number
     * 1021 = [318]twin prime with 1019. It is also a [319]Lucky prime.
     * 1022 = [320]Friedman number
     * [321]1023 = sum of five consecutive primes (193 + 197 + 199 + 211 +
       223);^[322][23] the number of [323]three-dimensional [324]polycubes
       with [325]7 cells;^[326][24] number of [327]elements in a
       [328]9-simplex; highest number one can count to on one's fingers
       using binary; magic number used in [329]Global Positioning System
       signals.
     * [330]1024 = 32^2 = 4^5 = 2^10, the number of [331]bytes in a
       [332]kilobyte (in 1999, the [333]IEC coined [334]kibibyte to use
       for 1024 with kilobyte being 1000, but this convention has not been
       widely adopted). 1024 is the smallest 4-digit square and also a
       [335]Friedman number.
     * 1025 = [336]Proth number 2^10 + 1; member of [337]Moser–de Bruijn
       sequence, because its base-4 representation (100001[4]) contains
       only digits 0 and 1, or it's a sum of distinct powers of 4 (4^5 +
       4^0); [338]Jacobsthal-Lucas number; hypotenuse of [339]primitive
       Pythagorean triangle
     * 1026 = sum of two distinct powers of 2 ([340]1024 + [341]2)
     * 1027 = sum of the squares of the first eight primes; can be written
       from base 2 to base 18 using only the digits 0 to 9.
     * 1028 = sum of totient function for first 58 integers; can be
       written from base 2 to base 18 using only the digits 0 to 9; number
       of primes <= 2^13.^[342][25]
     * 1029 = can be written from base 2 to base 18 using only the digits
       0 to 9.
     * 1030 = generalized heptagonal number
     * 1031 = exponent and number of ones for the fifth base-10
       [343]repunit prime,^[344][26] [345]Sophie Germain prime,^[346][13]
       [347]super-prime, [348]Chen prime
     * 1032 = sum of two distinct powers of 2 ([349]1024 + [350]8)
     * 1033 = [351]emirp, [352]twin prime with 1031
     * 1034 = sum of 12 positive 9th powers^[353][27]
     * 1035 = 45th [354]triangular number,^[355][28] [356]hexagonal
       number^[357][29]
     * 1036 = central polygonal number^[358][30]
     * 1037 = number in E-toothpick sequence^[359][31]
     * 1038 = [360]even [361]integer that is an unordered sum of two
       [362]primes in exactly n ways^[363][32]
     * 1039 = [364]prime of the form 8n+7,^[365][33] number of partitions
       of 30 that do not contain 1 as a part,^[366][34] [367]Chen prime,
       [368]Lucky prime
     * 1040 = 4^5 + 4^2: sum of distinct powers of 4.^[369][35] The number
       of pieces that could be seen in a 6 × 6 × 6× 6 Rubik's Tesseract.
     * 1041 = sum of 11 positive 5th powers^[370][36]
     * 1042 = sum of 12 positive 5th powers^[371][37]
     * 1043 = number whose sum of [372]even digits and sum of [373]odd
       digits are even^[374][38]
     * 1044 = sum of distinct powers of 4^[375][35]
     * 1045 = [376]octagonal number^[377][39]
     * 1046 = coefficient of f(q) (3rd order mock theta
       function)^[378][40]
     * 1047 = number of ways to split a strict composition of 18 into
       contiguous subsequences that have the same sum^[379][41]
     * 1048 = number of partitions of 27 into [380]squarefree
       parts^[381][42]
     * 1049 = [382]Sophie Germain prime,^[383][13] [384]highly cototient
       number,^[385][43] [386]Chen prime
     * 1050 = 1050[8] to [387]decimal becomes a [388]pronic number
       (552[10]),^[389][44] number of parts in all partitions of 29 into
       distinct parts^[390][45]
     * 1051 = [391]centered pentagonal number,^[392][46] [393]centered
       decagonal number
     * 1052 = sum of 9 positive 6th powers^[394][47]
     * 1053 = triangular matchstick number^[395][48]
     * 1054 = [396]centered triangular number^[397][49]
     * 1055 = sum of 12 positive 6th powers^[398][50]
     * 1056 = [399]pronic number^[400][51]
     * 1057 = central polygonal number^[401][52]
     * 1058 = sum of 4 positive 5th powers,^[402][53] area of a square
       with diagonal 46^[403][54]
     * 1059 = number n such that n^4 is written in the form of a sum of
       four positive 4th powers^[404][55]
     * 1060 = sum of the first twenty-five primes from [405]2 through
       [406]97 (the number of primes less than [407]100),^[408][56] and
       sixth sum of 10 consecutive primes, starting with [409]23 through
       [410]131.^[411][57]
     * 1061 = [412]emirp, [413]twin prime with 1063, number of prime
       numbers between 1000 and [414]10000 (or, number of four-digit
       primes in [415]decimal representation)^[416][58]
     * 1062 = number that is not the sum of two [417]palindromes^[418][59]
     * 1063 = [419]super-prime, sum of seven consecutive primes (137 + 139
       + 149 + 151 + 157 + 163 + 167); near-wall-sun-sun prime^[420][60].
       It is also a [421]twin prime with 1061.
     * 1064 = sum of two positive [422]cubes^[423][61]
     * 1065 = generalized duodecagonal^[424][62]
     * 1066 = number whose sum of their [425]divisors is a
       [426]square^[427][63]
     * 1067 = number of strict [428]integer [429]partitions of 45 in which
       are empty or have smallest [430]part not dividing the other
       ones^[431][64]
     * 1068 = number that is the sum of 7 positive 5th powers,^[432][7]
       total number of parts in all partitions of 15^[433][65]
     * 1069 = [434]emirp^[435][66]
     * 1070 = number that is the sum of 9 positive 5th powers^[436][67]
     * 1071 = [437]heptagonal number^[438][68]
     * 1072 = [439]centered heptagonal number^[440][69]
     * 1073 = number that is the sum of 12 positive 5th powers^[441][37]
     * 1074 = number that is not the sum of two [442]palindromes^[443][59]
     * 1075 = number non-sum of two [444]palindromes^[445][59]
     * 1076 = number of strict trees weight 11^[446][70]
     * 1077 = number where [447]7 outnumbers every other digit in the
       number^[448][71]
     * 1078 = [449]Euler transform of negative [450]integers^[451][72]
     * 1079 = every positive integer is the sum of at most 1079 tenth
       powers.
     * 1080 = pentagonal number,^[452][73] [453]largely composite
       number^[454][74]
     * 1081 = 46th [455]triangular number,^[456][28] member of
       [457]Padovan sequence^[458][75]
     * 1082 = central polygonal number^[459][30]
     * 1083 = three-quarter [460]square,^[461][76] number of partitions of
       53 into prime parts^[462][77]
     * 1084 = [463]third spoke of a hexagonal spiral,^[464][78]
       [465]1084^64 + 1 is prime
     * 1085 = number of partitions of n into distinct [466]parts > or =
       [467]2^[468][79]
     * 1086 = [469]Smith number,^[470][80] sum of totient function for
       first 59 integers
     * 1087 = super-prime, [471]cousin prime, [472]lucky prime^[473][81]
     * 1088 = octo-[474]triangular number, ([475]triangular number result
       being [476]136)^[477][82] sum of two distinct powers of 2,
       ([478]1024 + [479]64)^[480][83] number that is divisible by exactly
       seven [481]primes with the inclusion of [482]multiplicity^[483][84]
     * [484]1089 = 33^2, [485]nonagonal number, [486]centered octagonal
       number, first natural number whose digits in its decimal
       representation get reversed when multiplied by 9.^[487][85]
     * 1090 = sum of 5 positive 5th powers^[488][86]
     * 1091 = [489]cousin prime and [490]twin prime with 1093
     * 1092 = divisible by the number of primes below it
     * [491]1093 = the smallest [492]Wieferich prime (the only other known
       [493]Wieferich prime is 3511^[494][87]), [495]twin prime with 1091
       and [496]star number^[497][88]
     * 1094 = sum of 9 positive 5th powers,^[498][67] [499]1094^64 + 1 is
       prime
     * 1095 = sum of 10 positive 5th powers,^[500][89] number that is not
       the sum of two [501]palindromes
     * 1096 = hendecagonal number,^[502][90] number of strict solid
       partitions of 18^[503][91]
     * 1097 = [504]emirp,^[505][66] [506]Chen prime
     * 1098 = multiple of 9 containing digit 9 in its [507]base-10
       representation^[508][92]
     * 1099 = number where 9 outnumbers every other digit^[509][93]

1100 to 1199

   [[510]edit]

     * 1100 = number of partitions of 61 into distinct squarefree
       parts^[511][94]
     * 1101 = pinwheel number^[512][95]
     * 1102 = sum of totient function for first 60 integers
     * 1103 = [513]Sophie Germain prime,^[514][13] [515]balanced
       prime^[516][96]
     * 1104 = [517]Keith number^[518][97]
     * [519]1105 = 33^2 + 4^2 = 32^2 + 9^2 = 31^2 + 12^2 = 23^2 + 24^2,
       [520]Carmichael number,^[521][98] [522]magic constant of n × n
       normal [523]magic square and [524]n-queens problem for n = 13,
       [525]decagonal number,^[526][99] centered square number,^[527][14]
       [528]Fermat pseudoprime^[529][100]
     * 1106 = number of regions into which the plane is divided when
       drawing 24 ellipses^[530][101]
     * 1107 = number of non-isomorphic strict T[0] multiset partitions of
       weight 8^[531][102]
     * 1108 = [532]number k such that k^64 + 1 is prime
     * 1109 = Friedlander-Iwaniec prime,^[533][103] [534]Chen prime
     * 1110 = k such that 2^k + 3 is prime^[535][104]
     * 1111 = 11 × 101, palindrome that is a product of two palindromic
       primes,^[536][105] [537]repunit^[538][106]
     * 1112 = k such that 9^k - 2 is a prime^[539][107]
     * 1113 = number of strict partions of 40^[540][108]
     * 1114 = number of ways to write 22 as an orderless product of
       orderless sums^[541][109]
     * 1115 = number of partitions of 27 into a prime number of
       parts^[542][110]
     * 1116 = divisible by the number of primes below it
     * 1117 = number of diagonally symmetric polyominoes with 16
       cells,^[543][111] [544]Chen prime
     * 1118 = number of unimodular 2 × 2 matrices having all terms in
       {0,1,...,21}^[545][112]
     * 1119 = number of [546]bipartite graphs with 9 nodes^[547][113]
     * 1120 = [548]number k such that k^64 + 1 is prime
     * 1121 = number of squares between 34^2 and 34^4.^[549][114]
     * 1122 = pronic number,^[550][51] divisible by the number of primes
       below it
     * 1123 = [551]balanced prime^[552][96]
     * 1124 = [553]Leyland number^[554][115] using 2 & 10 (2^10 + 10^2),
       spy number
     * 1125 = [555]Achilles number
     * 1126 = number of 2 × 2 non-singular integer matrices with entries
       from {0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5}^[556][116]
     * 1127 = maximal number of pieces that can be obtained by cutting an
       annulus with 46 cuts^[557][117]
     * 1128 = 47th [558]triangular number,^[559][28] 24th hexagonal
       number,^[560][29] divisible by the number of primes below it
       ([561]188 × [562]6).^[563][118] 1128 is the dimensional

Prime numbers

   [[1746]edit]

   2000:^[1748][510]^[1749][511]

          1009, 1013, 1019, 1021, 1031, 1033, 1039, 1049, 1051, 1061,
